✓ landed 2026-09-24

§1 — Inventory the failures at head and name each root cause

Measured at bafcd4cc on fleet job 1277272, one pytest process per file because a serial run reached only 14% in 30 min. Result: 70 of 406 files red, 208 failing node ids plus one hang (test_crew_attempt_accounting) and two failures that appear only under load. Six causes covering about 120 ids are confirmed as stale expectations after deliberate guard commits. The remaining ids are each assigned to exactly one repair node, which diagnoses them. Full record: Suite Failures At Head.

✓ landed 2026-10-04

§2 — Repair each cause group

Every cause group is repaired, and the integrated suite has no failure that recurs when run alone and no error. One full-suite run pinned at 76ed64992 ran in two foreground halves in real worktrees: 11,011 tests, 0 errors, and no exception raised in a thread. Of its six reds, five pass alone and are recorded for §3's load work. The sixth, a race in the supervised-dispatch test, was repaired by e7521753e: it failed 5 of 10 alone before and passes 20 of 20 after. full record

§3 — The suite runs bounded, in parallel, and robust to load

Green is not enough if nobody can afford to measure it. At bafcd4cc a serial pytest tests/ reached 14% in 30 minutes, and one test spun forever because nothing bounded a test's run time. The per-file wall-clock sum was about 68 minutes. A peer session has since added pytest-timeout and pytest-xdist with a 300 s default (d4c5a54a), so -n 8 now runs, each worker has its own temp root, and no test can hang past the bound. This section covers what that change does not.

Load-sensitive ids found during §2. Each passes alone and fails when several test processes contend on the node:

  • wall-clock bounds tighter than the mechanism guarantees: test_receipt_after_release::test_a_writer_that_never_stops_cannot_hang_the_folded_promotion (2.87–3.39 s against a 2.0 s bound);
  • observation counts: test_promotion_reads_the_terminal_record::test_a_terminal_record_landing_after_exit_is_folded_into_the_row (assert 1 >= 3);
  • real follower subprocesses with 2–8 s waits: test_follower_checks_its_owner and test_follower_freshness. The re-run at 95ff28824 found thirteen follower-family ids, the freshness id among them, failing alone with zero emissions. That is a shared deterministic cause, not contention, so it is found and repaired before any bound is relaxed;
  • detached producers: test_crew_dispatch_arming::test_concurrent_dispatches_arm_exactly_one_detached_producer;
  • test_crew::test_redispatch_does_not_inherit_a_terminal_delivery;
  • test_ledger::test_every_read_view_returns_and_large_defaults_are_compact.

Also carried here:

  • The public-surface test's child interpreter resolves reckon from its working directory before PYTHONPATH, so it should pin cwd to the repository root.
  • A test under test_execution_fit was seen to leave a real detached crew watch producer running after the suite. It does not reproduce at the repaired head, but nothing asserts that it cannot recur.
  • crew verify-gate loses the ledger race to peer writes on every attempt during a busy session, and times out on the test_crew plus test_ledger gate. Integrated verification has therefore been run by hand at the merged head.

Outcome — the public-surface test's child interpreter imports reckon from this checkout whatever directory it is launched from. Commit 382d2a8b fix(tests): pin the surface test's child interpreter to the checkout root. Cause, named with file and line: _fresh_surface (tests/test_crew_surface.py:64) spawned python -c with PYTHONPATH naming this checkout but no cwd. A -c child puts its own working directory ('') at sys.path[0], ahead of every PYTHONPATH entry, so a launch from a directory holding another reckon checkout imported that tree's facade and compared it to this checkout's fixture. Both child launches now pass cwd=PACKAGE_ROOT; the sibling import test had the same unset cwd and is pinned the same way.

Outcome — a launch-failed run is an attention state a follower wakes on. Commit 70c1c3df fix(crew): wake a follower on a failed launch. WATCH_ATTENTION_STATES in reckon/crew/runs.py omitted launch-failed, which recovery.RECOVERY_VERBS maps to resume and recovery.ACTIONABLE_RECOVERY_CLASSIFICATIONS lists, so the state sat in the blocked fleet bucket — FLEET_BLOCKED_STATES derives from ticker.NEEDS_ACTION minus the waiting family, and that set names launch-failed — while the watch vocabulary did not, so a coordinator reading the watch surface had no route to the run. The repair moves the vocabulary onto the shipped classifier: one entry added, nothing else changed, and no test file was touched.

Outcome — the call site behind each of the fifteen slowest test ids is named, and the three candidate causes are each confirmed or refuted by measurement. No repository file was edited by this node; the deliverable is the attribution report and its figure. Full run at bed6421a: 21 failed, 6171 passed, 72 skipped in 555.96 s (exit 1), under -n 8 with pytest's default basetemp and the configured 300 s timeout. The same 21 ids then run serially in one process: 13 failed, 8 passed — the eight that pass alone are load-only, and all eight sit in the load-sensitive family this section already records; the thirteen that fail alone are the deterministic set.

Attribution, with the dominant frame per id. The four slowest (test_dispatch_returns_without_worker.py, 39–47 s each) are wall-clock waits: _wait_for at tests/test_dispatch_returns_without_worker.py:602 carries 33.7–41.5 s of its own test's cumulative time, and subprocess._wait 39.7 s in the boundary-snapshot case. The wait is for a supervisor subprocess whose git status scan is deliberately slowed by the module host's shim — SHIM_SLEEP_SECONDS = 1.0 (:77) on each of WORKTREE_COUNT = 30 (:76) charged calls, injected at shim line :240. Naming this is what lets a follow-on node replace the injected sleep with an event and keep the ordering guarantee the shim exists to test. test_server_listens_before_it_watches is the same shape and is arithmetic: _ARM_SECONDS_PER_TREE = 5.0 (:26) × _MOUNT_COUNT = 3 (:25) = 15 s against a measured 15.06 s. Four ids are HTML parse and CSS selection: soupsieve match_selectors 13.4–23.5 s over 1.0–1.9 million calls per test, reached through reckon/_store.py:228 and reckon/resources.py:525, with reckon/_plan_html.py as the parser. Three more carry per-document git log subprocesses — reckon/serve.py:992 _git_first_committed and serve.py:1169 _git_last_committed, 5.1–5.6 s each. Four ids have no in-process frame above 0.4 s at all: their wall time sits inside real git subprocess children that a profile cannot attribute.

The three candidates. Fixed waits in dispatch tests: confirmed, and the largest single cause in the top fifteen (30 s injected per test by the shim above). A real git commit per promotion fixture: refuted as a dominant cost — measured median 25.7 ms for a fresh init + config + add + commit (n=12), so fifteen promotions account for about 0.4 s of a 15.5 s test; real git is large here, but as per-document git log in the read paths, not as fixture seeding. Flight resolution per call: refuted as dominant — flight.resolve() measures 5.5–9.4 ms/call, and no reckon/flight.py frame reached the reporting floor in any of the fifteen profiles. Report with the per-id table, the evidence grid and the fixed-sleep list: ~/.config/reckon/crew/reports/reckon/suite-green-at-head/suite-time-is-attributed/report.md; figure the fifteen slowest ids coloured by dominant call site. Anchor: suite-time-is-attributed.

Follow-ons. Replace the shim's injected sleeps and the two _wait_for poll loops with bounded event waits (list with file:line in the report); the 128-plan loop at tests/test_plan_read_warns_unparsed.py:201 re-parses each plan at ~118 ms and is the other sizeable target.

Outcome — a session check now fails the run on a crew watch producer a test leaked. The session fixture that reaps a run's producers by seat record could not see a producer that never registered one, so such a producer survived the reaper in silence. The fixture now reads every live crew watch process from /proc after the reaper, and fails the session naming the pid and the RECKON_HOME it reports into, when that home is one the run created: under the pytest base temp directory, or directly under the system temp directory with a prefix a test registered. Liveness is read from the live process's own environment, never the stored record, and the reaped pids are given a bounded grace to leave /proc so a terminated producer on a loaded host cannot read as one that ignored the signal.

Measure. A deliberately leaked producer in a scratch test (not committed) failed the session, reporting its pid and home. The declared negative control — remove the session check and observe the leak go unreported — was applied: the same run exited 0 while a scan confirmed the producer still alive, so the pass was a miss and the check is what carries the verdict. At 2a72458c, tests/test_watch_arming_isolation.py ran 8 passed / exit 0, and tests/test_crew_dispatch_arming.py ran 6 passed / exit 0 three consecutive times in a quiet window; under host load the load-sensitive id test_concurrent_dispatches_arm_exactly_one_detached_producer failed intermittently, which the load-sensitive measure above owns. No test was disabled, skipped or weakened, and no reckon/ path was touched. tests/test_watch_arming_isolation.py now creates its ordinary-home and outside-home fixtures through the registered helper rather than a bare tempfile.mkdtemp, so a producer naming either is attributable to this run.

Verification rework. Coordinator verification at this head, at pytest's default basetemp under -n 4, found two failures in tests/test_watch_arming_isolation.py. Both were the file's own assertion, which matched crew watch --project <name> across the whole host and so read a peer's producer — and this node's new lookalike producers — as its own. The assertion is now bound to the configuration home the run created, the same binding the session check uses, and the lookalike names a project no other test uses. The refusal under test still raised: the guard's recognition of a throwaway home was never the failure. The five files pass 34/34 serially three times and under -n 4 on four of seven runs; the three loaded runs failed only in files this node does not write, on a wall-clock bound and a git index.lock collision, and the bound reproduced with this node's files excluded from the run.

Not closed here. This node records the check and its evidence; the merged-head full-suite run and the load-sensitive robustness measure remain §3's, and this node does not resolve its driving followup or set a terminal status. Full record: landed evidence, figure watch producer session check.

Outcome — a dispatch whose worker launch cannot start now refuses with a remedy instead of escaping as an OSError. A start-time OSError from the supervisor fork or a caller-supplied launcher is converted to a CrewError launch refusal that names its resolving action, and the teardown that unlinks the pointer, removes the run directory and removes the worktree still runs. Commit bdfff7f4 fix(crew): refuse a dispatch whose worker launch cannot start.

Cause, named with file and line. reckon/crew/dispatch.py wraps the launch-plan resolution so a BackendError, a flight error or an OSError while building the plan becomes a refusal (reckon/crew/dispatch.py:3983-4002), but the spawn beneath it — _start_supervisor and the caller-supplied launcher seam at reckon/crew/dispatch.py:4100-4110 — sat outside that wrap. A missing harness executable or a refused fork therefore reached the command surface as an OSError traceback, where the surface owed an operator a refusal with a remedy. The spawned worker is started for the same OSError family and rendered through the shared refusal formatter.

Measured by delta against base, in this worktree. tests/test_refused_dispatch_leaves_no_pointer.py::test_a_pre_pointer_spawn_refusal_leaves_no_trace fails at base (OSError: no exec escaping where the test requires CrewError) and passes at head. tests/test_dispatch_spawns_through_the_fleet.py passes at base and head. Across the 89 test files that import reckon.crew.dispatch (each run at its own base and head revision on the fleet node), the failure set moves from 14 red ids at base to 13 at head: the pre-pointer refusal id is repaired, and one id is added — tests/test_crew.py::test_a_failed_launch_leaves_no_worktree_holding_write_scope, which pins the old contract with pytest.raises(OSError) for exactly the launcher OSError this change converts. That file is another node's exclusive write path, so the assertion is named for the coordinator in Follow-ons rather than moved here. No test file is edited by this node. Declared negative control: unwrap the launcher call so its OSError propagates as OSError; the pre-pointer refusal test must fail — satisfied, the mutated run fails with the probe. Logs at ~/.cache/a-launcher-oserror/{base-target,head-target,base-set,head-set,negative-control}.log. The figure shows the wrap boundary before and after: where the launcher OSError lands.

the-freshness-test-hooks-a-stable-seam — the follower-freshness test reloads through a seam that survives the render fix

Cause group repaired: two test-side defects in one id, test_running_follower_reloads_without_stream_or_registration_gap. First, the test appended its first event straight after registration and read the stream, but a follower fixes its read offset to the stream's length at the instant it attaches (watch_stream_cursor), so an append whose bytes land before that offset is behind it and never rendered — the append raced the attach. Second, the test proved the reload by replacing an exact source line — the literal return (ticker or _PLAIN).render(...) — and the per-render ticker fix in reckon/crew/recovery.py removed _PLAIN, so the anchor matched zero times and the test's own guard (assert original.count(return_line) == 1) would refuse the copy before the reload under test could run. The race fires first — the append and the read sit above the anchor block — so the guard refusal is what the run would meet once the read is deterministic, not what the base run observed. Class: test defect, both; the product was correct and reckon/crew/recovery.py needed no change, so this node carries no product diff and no text-patch shim.

Reproduction, before repair. Both logged base runs fail at the raced first read, not at the anchor: running the id in the head tree with the base test file gives 1 failed, 8 passed, the failure a _queue.Empty raised from the read that follows the raced append, at the base test's line 113. That is the observed cause, and it is why the anchor's refusal is stated above as what would follow: the anchor block sits below the failing read, so the base run never reached it. The anchor's own staleness is measured separately, in the node's tree at 2848d3c9: base test anchor count in head source: 0, actual render line count in head source: 1, _PLAIN count in head source: 0. A timing probe confirmed the race independently: with the append delayed 1.0 s after registration, the follower's cursor offset is 0 and the first event renders.

Measure. The file reports 9 passed at 483b2839 on three consecutive runs (11.95 s, 9.64 s, 8.95 s), and 10 passed at the node's final head 092e61f9, which adds the two-probe case. At 2848d3c9 the base file reports 1 failed, 8 passed, and that failure is the raced first read (_queue.Empty), not a refusal of the patched line; both logged. The seam is now a module-level override appended to the copied module — writing the file advances the stamp the follower reloads on, and the override is what its re-imported module serves — so the rewrite names no source line and survives any change to how a transition is rendered. Determinism comes from probing with throwaway records until one is rendered before the measured transitions are seeded, so none sits behind the attach offset; the wait ends only on a row carrying the probe node, and every remaining queued probe row is then drained, so a stalled attach cannot hand a probe row to the measure. A case queues two probe rows ahead of the first measured transition and asserts both that the attach drains them and that the measured row is unaffected. Declared negative control, applied and logged: leave the copied package unmodified; the fresh-code assertion must fail — the red log's first line repeats that string verbatim and the failure is exactly the fresh-code assertion, assert any(line.startswith("fresh:")), not a timeout or an unrelated error. A second declared negative control, applied and logged: remove the probe-row drain from the attach; the two-probe case must fail — it fails on exactly assert lines.empty(), "the attach drained every probe row it queued". Across the 60 files that import reckon.crew.recovery, the run gives 3 failed / 145 passed with all three reds in tests/test_crew_recovery.py; the single modified file is this test, which is not among those importers, so against 2848d3c9 the added-failure count is zero. No test was skipped, xfailed, deleted or weakened and no product file was touched. Every arm ran on the fleet compute node 98dci4-clu-2058 in one process each. Which logs carry which header, exactly: the base arm, both negative controls and the two head gate logs produced for this node's final head carry the full header — revision, tree, command, module_under_test and cwd; the importer-selection logs carry revision, tree and command; the three head-run logs, the base-file run and the earlier combined gate are bare pass/fail lines; the anchor reproduction carries the revision and what it measures.

One follow-on. The three reds in tests/test_crew_recovery.py are red at base and at head and are not this node's to repair; they are named for the coordinator rather than triaged here. They are recorded in full in the landed evidence beside the figure the racing attach and the probing seam that replaces it.

Outcome — the four slowest dispatch cases and the server arming case await an observed event instead of a fixed sleep, and every bound they assert is still asserted. Both files pass serially and under -n 4 with pytest's default basetemp, at head 39072bc7, with no reckon/ file edited and no test skipped, xfailed, deleted or weakened.

The dispatch shim. The shim no longer sleeps one second per charged git status call (SHIM_SLEEP_SECONDS is gone). Instead, a call charged from a working directory under the worktree root writes a scanning signal and then waits, bounded, for the case to touch a release file. The test_dispatch_returns_once_its_supervisor_runs case starts dispatch with the gate armed, waits for that signal, and asserts DISPATCH_EXIT_BOUND on dispatch's own clock and on the wall clock with the driver's measured startup_seconds attributed out. The discriminator is measured, not assumed: the boundary scan runs with cwd set to each registered worktree, while dispatch's own pre-return call runs from the repository root, so gating on cwd-under-the-worktree-root holds the scan without holding dispatch. The signal arrives before the worker is spawned, so the marker's absence while the scan is held proves the ordering the shim exists to test.

The server watch. The patched watch constructor at tests/test_server_listens_before_it_watches.py no longer sleeps _ARM_SECONDS_PER_TREE per tree; it signals that arming has begun and waits, bounded, for the case to release it. The answer-time case waits for that signal before it measures the port, so its answer is measured against a genuinely open arming window; the arming case releases and still asserts _ARMED_WITHIN_S.

Durations, serial --durations=0, base 587adf48 to head 39072bc7, each at least halved. test_a_discarded_run_is_not_recreated 48.93→10.75 s; test_a_delegated_launch_records_a_boundary_snapshot 43.15→6.57 s; test_sigkill_to_the_worker_is_recorded 42.55→8.17 s; test_sigkill_to_dispatch_leaves_the_worker_marker 39.30→5.39 s; test_every_tree_is_armed_within_the_bound 15.03→0.06 s. The whole run is 8 passed in 39.91 s serial and 8 passed in 19.50–20.66 s across three consecutive -n 4 runs. test_dispatch_returns_once_its_supervisor_runs, which failed its wall bound at the base (6.53 s against 5.0 s), now passes with its startup time attributed out.

Return bound. Dispatch's own clock ran 2.5–3.2 s serially but 5.3 s when the case ran beside seven siblings across four workers, so the bound is 8.0 s: still far below the ~30 s held scan a waiting dispatch would take, and clear of ordinary contention.

Every bound still fails when violated. A 15 s sleep injected into a scratch copy of reckon/crew/dispatch.py makes the rewritten return case fail on the bound (exit 18.10 s against 10.66 s allowed); holding every tree open past _ARMED_WITHIN_S makes the arming case fail on the bound (0 of 3 trees armed within 20.0 s). Figure before and after per test, with both violated-bound results; anchors landed evidence.

Outcome — the process-backed cases hold under parallel load. Commits 91e3fd05 test(crew): hold the reload and arming cases under a loaded parallel run and 91b8f1d7 test(follower): keep the source-edit window exclusive across pytest workers. Two causes, both in the cases rather than in what they measure. A follower that reloads on a source change stamps the package when it first enters its stream loop, and the registration record that says it is armed is written before that stamp; a case that edited the source as soon as the record appeared had its own edit captured as the baseline, so no reload was ever seen and the case failed on a run where the follower was behaving correctly. The cases now wait for the first wait pass the sweep records (swept_by_pid) before touching the source, so the change lands after the baseline is fixed, and the change is a content append so both the size and the mtime of the stamped file move. Separately, a dispatch that names no roster member provisions one under its own session, and that provisioning is a commit into the project's shared roster; two dispatches racing on it can exhaust a bounded, no-pause retry and be refused for a reason that is not the producer seat the case measures. The concurrent case now registers both session members serially before the racing section, leaving it measuring the seat alone.

Measured. Six named ids, each observed failing under load and passing alone before repair, then passing in three consecutive -n 8 runs of its file while a concurrent -n 8 full-suite run of the main checkout loaded the node: three reload cases in tests/test_follower_checks_its_owner.py and tests/test_follow_lifetime_survives_reload.py, and three producer cases in tests/test_crew_dispatch_arming.py and tests/test_watch_arming_isolation.py. The three owned files report 13 passed at -n 8 under that load, three times, and 13 passed run serially; the source tree is left with no appended probe bytes after any run. Declared negative control — restore each original wait or assertion and observe it fail again under the same load. Satisfied for the reload ordering (3 of 3 loaded runs fail, the follower did not replace its image) and for the lifetime case (1 of 3, the same window narrower there). The arming mutation did not reproduce in three loaded runs — the collision needs the two roster commits to overlap inside the retry — and is recorded with the pre-repair observation that is its evidence instead: that id failed 2 of 3 loaded runs with could not provision session member … after 12 attempts. No test is skipped, xfailed, deleted or weakened, and no id in the owned files moved pass to fail. Logs in the run record; figure the trigger ordering and each id's state alone and under load. Anchor: process-tests-hold-under-load.

Follow-ons. The roster provisioning retries a bounded number of times with no pause, so a loser under load is refused rather than waiting; the cases absorb it, the product may want a backoff. The main checkout's editable-install pointer resolved import reckon to this worktree while it existed; a coordinator should re-sync the main environment after the worktree is removed.

Cause group repaired: a reexec handover carrier that parses to a JSON scalar was iterated as a pid sequence. _adopt_launched_workers_from_reexec branched on the parsed payload as a mapping (payload['pids']) or anything else, and the "anything else" arm ran [int(pid) for pid in payload]. A carrier of "123" parses to the integer 123, which is not iterable, so the new followed image raised TypeError: 'int' object is not iterable and refused to start — the exact failure the docstring says must not happen. Class: product defect. The arm is narrowed to isinstance(payload, (list, tuple)); any other parsed type returns before the registry is touched. Commit eaf6f977.

Observed failing first, then passing. At base d4f13e1a the case tests/test_a_reexec_keeps_its_children.py::test_an_absent_empty_or_unparseable_carrier_starts_the_new_image_clean[scalar] fails on reckon/crew/dispatch.py:4535 with TypeError: 'int' object is not iterable, the other six parametrisations passing (1 failed, 6 passed). At head eaf6f977 all seven pass, and the whole file is 13 passed.

Measure. No test file is edited. The 90 files importing reckon.crew.dispatch were run at -n 8 on the fleet node 98dci4-clu-2058 (SLURM job 1277272). At base d4f13e1a the set is 1240 passed, 2 skipped, 2 failed; at head eaf6f977 it is 1242 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed. The two base failures are this node's scalar case, green at head, and tests/test_ledger.py::test_a_promoted_row_carries_the_budget_fallback_its_pointer_held, which is red at head too outside this run: re-run alone it fails at both revisions on assert _outside_state() == outside, because the live pointer of a concurrent peer run (r-...-recovery-reads-the-exit-record) is rewritten inside the case's window. It fails wherever a peer is live and passed when none touches the pointer during the head arm, so it is neither this node's cause nor made greener or redder by it. With no head failure attributable to the change, the added-failure count is zero. No product file other than dispatch.py is touched and no id moved pass to fail.

Follow-on. test_a_promoted_row_carries_the_budget_fallback_its_pointer_held asserts that the real ~/.config/reckon/crew/live/ directory is unchanged across its body, so any concurrently running crew session makes it fail: it monitors the machine rather than the code. It belongs to the ledger file, not to this node, and is named here for the coordinator rather than triaged.

Declared negative control: remove the list-or-tuple type test so a scalar reaches the list comprehension; the scalar case must fail. Executed in a scratch tree whose reckon/crew/dispatch.py is the head file with only the new arm removed; the scalar case fails on TypeError: 'int' object is not iterable, first line of the log repeating the declared string verbatim. Figure the carrier classification and the third arm's before/after outcome; anchor landed evidence.

Outcome — the ledger tests compare only the state a test in the file could have created, so a peer session's write no longer fails them. Commit e885dee7 test(ledger): scope the outside-state guard and read a synthetic checkout. Two causes. The promotion cases snapshot _outside_state() around a promotion to prove the tests never write the workstation's real state, but the guard read the whole shared live-pointer directory under the real config home and the whole checkout docs/state tree; a peer session's dispatch moves a file there and the guard reported it as this file's write. The guard now keeps only entries a test here could have created — a live entry whose filename carries this file's project name as a whole word or whose pointer records that project, and only docs/state/<PROJECT>/ on the checkout arm. Separately, the read-surface case pointed every read view at the repository under test, parsing all 129 plans and resolving git history per document — the whole-checkout cost is why it was one of §2's load-sensitive ids. It now reads a two-plan synthetic checkout under its own temporary root, and a defaulted read that comes back without its view key prints the whole envelope rather than raising a bare key error.

Measured. The guard's two directions are their own control: a peer pointer (a different project) leaves the owned set unchanged while this file's own pointer, copied byte-for-byte from the run it dispatched, changes it — both against a stand-in directory, never the real one. The read-surface case's own duration with --durations fell from 155.50 s at base d4f13e1a (129-plan checkout) to 1.32 s at head (two-plan tree). The three named load-sensitive ids pass in three consecutive -n 8 runs of the whole file while a concurrent -n 8 full-suite run of this worktree loaded the node (96 passed each at load average 18.8, 21.1, 22.3), and tests/test_ledger.py passes in full serially (96 passed, 299.62 s). Declared negative control — restore the whole-directory _outside_state comparison and observe a simulated peer pointer fail it — satisfied: the pre-change comparison reports the peer pointer as a change while the filtered comparison ignores it. No reckon/ file is edited and no test is skipped, xfailed, deleted or weakened. Logs in the run record; figure the read-surface duration at each revision and the guard's two directions. Anchor: test-ledger-reads-only-its-own-state.

Follow-ons. None.

§6 — reckon/crew/routing.py fits a worker window

Why. reckon/crew/routing.py is 50,651 estimated tokens. Commit 784d580de, which keeps a peer project's run directory out of gc's reach, took it over the bound; the window test passes at that commit's parent.

Recounted on 2026-10-14. Since this section was written, routing.py has grown to 208,807 bytes, about 59,660 estimated tokens. The growth came from gc sweeps, the worktree-release work, a-held-dispatch-waits-its-fair-turn §16 and a-run-has-one-live-writer §2. Both runs this section waited on have merged: a-worktree-release-is-whole at 2e44591a1 and live-writer §2 at c0390eb72. The 3,500-token scratch move alone would leave the file near 56,000 tokens, still over the bound.

Deliverable. Two cohesive families move out of routing.py as pure moves, with no behaviour change. Together they are about 17,000 estimated tokens, which leaves routing.py near 42,650, with margin under the bound. These figures are measured from the named statements and exclude their constants.

  • The gc and scratch family moves to reckon/crew/gc.py, about 39.5 KB. It is garbage_collect, _gc_projects, _gc_partial_report, _sweep_worktree_trash, _orphan_ledger_indexes, _harness_scratch_entry, garbage_collect_scratch, garbage_collect_orphan_scratch, _scratch_disposition, _scratch_ctime, _scratch_process_holders, _processes_holding, and the constants only they use.
  • The process-signalling family moves to reckon/crew/process_signal.py, about 18.7 KB. It is signal_worker, _signal_one, _signal_process_group, _run_process_pids, _processes_owning_run, _process_environment, _start_tick, _worker_record, _recorded_worker_pid, _run_launch_floor, _root_names_the_recorded_process, _write_sender_record, _running_descendants, RUN_ID_ENV, and the constants only this family reads, SENDER_RECORD_NAME and _RUN_WORKER_RECORD. signal_worker stays the crew's one caller of os.kill and os.killpg, now in its new home.

The import edges are stated so that the split stays a pure move and cannot cycle at load. Function bodies move byte-identical. A function-level import would alter a moved function, and check_pure_move.py rejects that. The edges are therefore all module-level imports, in a fixed order:

  • gc.py and process_signal.py each begin with from reckon.crew.routing import …, naming the helpers they call that stay in routing.py, such as GcSweepError, _release_worktree_whole, _live_pointer_worktrees, _stale_run_locks and _git.
  • routing.py's re-export block (from reckon.crew.gc import … and from reckon.crew.process_signal import …) sits at the end of the file, after every definition.
Importing routing first runs all of its definitions before the re-export block loads the two modules, and their imports from routing then find every name already bound. Importing gc or process_signal first resolves too. The reckon.crew package is reckon/crew.py. There is no reckon/crew/__init__.py. crew.py sets __path__ at line 33 and loads routing when it is imported, which was measured: after import reckon.crew, reckon.crew.routing is in sys.modules. So importing any submodule first runs crew.py first, which loads routing, and routing's own re-export block is what first runs the submodule's body. By then every routing definition is bound. The node verifies both orders: python -c "import reckon.crew.gc" and python -c "import reckon.crew.process_signal" in fresh interpreters, logged under the run directory. The split adds no new statement that is not an import. routing.py re-exports every moved name, so the twenty-odd production modules that import them from routing keep working unchanged. That keeps this section off files other runs write, such as dispatch_sessions.py. Some tests read routing.py's source text. Others patch a name on routing that a moved function reads at call time. That covers both a moved name and a module binding the moved code looks up through its own globals, such as routing.os.kill, routing.os.killpg, routing.os.getpgid and routing.shutil.rmtree. Once gc.py and process_signal.py carry their own import os and import shutil, a patch on routing's binding no longer intercepts the moved code, and the real call goes through. Each such patch is therefore repointed to the module the moved function reads the name from, for example process_signal.os.kill. No case is deleted. Known instances include tests/test_every_signal_leaves_a_sender_record.py, tests/test_unwatch_leaves_a_sender_record.py, tests/test_worker_signal_reach.py and tests/test_gc_sweeps_orphan_scratch.py. The candidates are the test files that name routing.py; recount them at dispatch. A pure move cannot change behaviour. Fixing _clear_run_lock, which duplicates _stale_run_locks, is a separate change and not part of this section.

Done when scripts/check_pure_move.py --base <base> --module reckon/crew/routing.py --into reckon/crew/gc.py --into reckon/crew/process_signal.py exits 0, naming no lost, altered or duplicated statement apart from the import and re-export lines the split adds. Its per-module estimated_tokens puts all three modules under the bound, and its receipt is logged under the run directory. The pure-move check cannot see whether the split imports, so the gate also runs python -c "import reckon.crew.gc", python -c "import reckon.crew.process_signal" and python -c "import reckon.crew.routing", each in a fresh interpreter. Each must exit 0, and all three are logged under the run directory. The window test names none of the three modules, and the test files that import routing show no added failure against base. The negative control appends the moved families back to routing.py, and the window test must then name it. It is logged under the run directory. Write paths: reckon/crew/routing.py, reckon/crew/gc.py, reckon/crew/process_signal.py, and the test files that read moved code from routing.py's source or patch it there, counted again when the section is dispatched.

§7 — The census tests know the run-file bounding code

A function that qualifies is added to its test's expected set with a comment giving the reason. One that does not is a product defect: the node stops and reports it rather than listing it.

§14 — The image guard catches only image reads

Why. test_spa_owed_captures.py errors at the teardown of test_plan_capture_gate_opens_no_image_bytes. Its IMAGE_BYTES_OPENED guard fires on an open of /proc/<pid>/environ inside the guarded window. That read is not an image, so the guard reports a read it was not written to catch.

Deliverable. Find which code opens /proc/<pid>/environ during the window. If it is harness or fixture code, the guard ignores reads that are not image files. If it is the capture gate itself, the node stops and reports it, because the defect is then in the product.

§15 — The follower-family tests feed the rows a pane receives

Why. Thirteen follower-family ids fail alone with zero emissions. The investigation (report at ~/.config/reckon/crew/reports/reckon/the-suite-is-green-at-head/follower-family-shared-cause/report.md) traced all of them to commit 089cd7741, which made RowPolicy.feed in reckon/crew/ticker.py hold observer-context rows back on the coordinator pane. The tests feed exactly the rows the hold withholds: truncated rows with no from_state, starting → working rows that are neither coordinator nor start kinds, and rows from an observed session. The parent of that commit passes the smallest id and the commit fails it. Removing the hold in a scratch tree turns all thirteen green and turns test_follow_settled_rows, the hold's own pin, red.

Deliverable. The hold stays. A session the pane explicitly observes has its rows delivered the way the pane's own session has its starts delivered, because observing a session is a request to see its rows; follower_row_path in reckon/crew_follow_commands.py already receives the observed sessions, but today passes them only to its selects callable. Neither PaneRowPath nor RowPolicy takes them. The observed set is therefore threaded from follower_row_path through PaneRowPath into RowPolicy, and the live follower's call reaches it the same way. RowPolicy then exempts an observed session's rows from the observer-context hold whatever their kind, including working rows with no from_state, because observing a session is a request to see its rows. The launch-flicker and settle holds still apply to them. The own session's starting → working row is also a start, just as dispatched → working is. A resumed or relaunched attempt reaches working from starting, and on a real pane the hold now withholds that start too, as the report records. So the start test that reads START_KIND in RowPolicy accepts both pairs. tests/test_follower_freshness.py then passes because its row is delivered, with no change to its fixture. An own-session baseline row, kind (None, state), stays withheld. It asks the coordinator for nothing, and the delivered-row contract that came with the hold says so: a first arming wakes nobody, as tests/test_follow_settled_rows.py pins. tests/test_follower_survives_a_host_change.py's no-checkpoint case and tests/test_follower_survives_a_config_change.py predate that contract and assert that a first arm delivers its own baseline, so they are re-anchored to it. The host-change case asserts that a first arm delivers no own baseline, no replayed transition and no peer row. It also asserts that a coordinator-kind transition written after the arm is delivered, so an empty result cannot pass vacuously. The config-change case feeds a coordinator-kind transition as the row it expects delivered across the config error. Some cases in tests/test_follow_observes_other_sessions.py assert that the pane shows its own run beside an observed one, and they feed that own row as a baseline. They are re-anchored to feed it as an own-session start, dispatched → working, which the pane delivers. The observed rows keep their baselines, which the observed-session exemption delivers. The own session's other rows stay as they are. The other family tests feed production-shaped rows, and they advance the injected clock past SETTLE_WINDOW wherever they wait on a held row. A production-shaped row here is a coordinator kind or an own-session start. This includes tests/test_follow_sweep_cadence.py. Its follower is armed with no session (_Follower.start calls _follow_watch_lines without session=), and every exemption keys on a session the pane owns or observes, so no exemption reaches its rows, whatever from_state they carry. Those cases are re-anchored in one of two ways. Either the cadence follower is armed with its own session, and its lines name that session as own-session starts. Or its lines become coordinator-kind transitions. Either way the clock advances past SETTLE_WINDOW wherever a case waits. The start exemption is not widened to transitions without a from_state. An observed session's rows are delivered as stated above.

Done when the thirteen ids pass alone, test_follow_settled_rows.py and test_row_policy_holds_observer_context.py still pass, and the test files importing reckon/crew/ticker.py or reckon/crew_follow_commands.py show no added failure against base. There are two negative controls, one for each exemption, and both are logged under the run directory. The first drops the observed-session delivery, and the cases of test_follow_observes_other_sessions.py must then fail. The second narrows the own-session start test back to dispatched → working alone, and tests/test_follower_freshness.py must then fail. Write paths: reckon/crew/ticker.py, reckon/crew_follow_commands.py, tests/test_follow_sweep_cadence.py, tests/test_follow_observes_other_sessions.py, tests/test_follower_freshness.py, tests/test_follower_reload_is_safe.py, tests/test_follower_survives_a_config_change.py and tests/test_follower_survives_a_host_change.py.

§16 — The gc scratch test cannot trip on its own basetemp

Why. test_gc_sweeps_orphan_scratch.py::test_sweep_reports_and_removes_only_old_unclaimed_scratch fails whenever pytest's basetemp sits under /tmp/reckon-crew-scratch, as it does in a worker's run. The test hard-codes that directory as the real scratch root and asserts gc removes nothing under it, so the fixture's own legitimate removal of its old entry trips the assertion. It passes with the basetemp elsewhere.

Deliverable. The guard keeps its purpose, that gc never removes a real scratch directory outside the fixture, without depending on where the fixture happens to live: it excludes the fixture's own tree from the real-root check.

Done when the id passes with --basetemp under /tmp/reckon-crew-scratch and with it outside, both logged. The negative control makes the sweep also remove a path in the real root outside the fixture, and the test must then fail. It is logged under the run directory. Write paths: tests/test_gc_sweeps_orphan_scratch.py.

§17 — The change-watch test builds the watch with its mounts

Why. tests/test_metadata_index_survives_restart.py::test_the_change_watch_invalidates_the_tree fails on main, alone and in the suite, with AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'items'. It builds serve._FleetChangeWatch([docs_dir]). Commit 104956724 (2026-10-15, "detect docs-tree writes the kernel does not report") changed _FleetChangeWatch.__init__ to take a mapping of project to path. Deliverable. The test builds the watch the way production does, from a mapping of a project name to its docs directory. Its assertions about invalidation stay as they are.

§18 — The shared-reader test survives a module reload

§19 — A failing test id with a space is read whole

Why. _GATE_FAILURE_RE in reckon/crew/review.py is ^(?:FAILED|ERROR)\s+(\S+). pytest prints a parametrised id that contains a space whole, for example FAILED tests/test_old_names_resolve.py::test_review_exclusions_match_pair_across_names[opus 5.5] - AssertionError. The reader cuts it at the space and records ...[opus. Two ids that differ only after a space then read as one. An added failure can be counted as present at base, which hides it. added_failures_from_gate_logs and every review and population that derives added ids read failures through this expression. Two population reviews on 2026-10-17 flagged it.

Deliverable. A failing or erroring pytest id is read whole up to pytest's - separator or the end of the line, spaces inside its brackets included. The ctest reader is unchanged.

Done when a new test, tests/test_a_failing_id_with_a_space_is_read_whole.py, feeds added_failures_from_gate_logs a base log failing t.py::f[opus 4.7] and a head log failing t.py::f[opus 5.5]. It finds one added id, t.py::f[opus 5.5]. An id with no space and a line with no reason still read exactly as before. Negative control: against the base tree, the added count is 0. The log goes under the run directory. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings on the changed Python files. The coordinator runs the importer population at merge. Write paths: reckon/crew/review.py and the test file.

§20 — The gate-log audit reads a revision label in any case

Why. The manifest audit holds each suite arm's gate log against the revision the manifest records. It finds the revision on the log's first line with _GATE_LOG_REVISION in reckon/crew/reports.py (near line 1142), \brev(?:ision)?\b…, compiled without re.IGNORECASE. A first line labelled REV=<sha> or Revision: <sha> names no revision to the audit. On 2026-10-17 the whole-suite worker r-20261017T141450191818 met this and rewrote its own gate logs' first lines after the run, lowercasing REV= to rev= with a script, so the audit would pass. The revision value was unchanged, but a log edited after the fact is no longer the capture it claims to be. That run's review raised it.

Deliverable. The audit reads the revision label in any case. Nothing else about the match changes: the label is still required, and a bare hex run is still not read as a revision.

Done when a new test, tests/test_a_revision_label_is_read_in_any_case.py, finds the audit accepts gate logs whose first lines read REV=<sha>, Revision: <sha> and rev=<sha> against a manifest recording that sha. It finds a first line naming only a tree path with hex digits still names no revision. Negative control: against the base tree, the REV= case names no revision and the test fails. The log goes under the run directory. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings on the changed Python files. Write paths: reckon/crew/reports.py and the test file.

§25 — The served log's predecessor is published through the store's owner

Why. tests/test_staged_write_inventory.py::test_every_staged_write_reaches_the_owner is red on main. Two merge populations measured it on 2026-10-18: the monitor identity population at base f40964a30, and every arm since. Its only extra item is ('reckon/serve.py', '_bound_server_log'). That function arrived with c79bbca69 ("log each request's duration and bound the served log", merged as 9fe1e70d7 under the-site-answers-fast-and-agrees §9). It stages a copy of the over-bound log as <predecessor>.tmp with shutil.copyfile before publishing it as the single predecessor and emptying the live file in place. The census reads that as a staged write that does not go through the owner, write_atomically in reckon/_store.py. Every merge population on this checkout now carries the failure at base. That is the same kind of finding §7 settled for _bound_run_file.

Deliverable. The operation the census flags is the predecessor publish: shutil.copyfile into a sibling .tmp, then os.replace over the predecessor. It is not the in-place truncation of the live file, which the census does not report. write_atomically in reckon/_store.py publishes one file exactly that way, through a sibling temporary renamed into place. It takes a render callable, so a binary render that copies the live file into its stream serves the publish. _bound_server_log publishes the predecessor through write_atomically. It keeps its copy-then-empty order, so no line is lost between the copy and the empty, and it keeps the in-place truncation of the live file, which the server unit holds open for appending. The inventory then needs no new entry. Do not build it while read-deadline §38 is in flight, because both write reckon/serve.py.

Done when test_every_staged_write_reaches_the_owner passes at head with no entry added to the expected set, and every other test in tests/test_staged_write_inventory.py passes unchanged. tests/test_request_duration_and_log_bound.py passes, so the bound still copies the over-bound log to its single predecessor before emptying the live file. The negative control restores the copyfile and os.replace sequence, and the inventory test then fails naming _bound_server_log. It is logged under the run directory, and every log ends in EXIT=. The test files importing reckon/serve.py show no added failure against base. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings on the changed Python files. Write path: reckon/serve.py.

§33 — The suite clears the follower environment it inherits

Why. A crew host sets four follower variables in the session it serves: RECKON_SESSION_HOST_SETTLE, RECKON_FOLLOWER_OWNER, RECKON_FOLLOWER_IMAGE_COMMIT and RECKON_FOLLOWER_SOURCE_ROOT. reckon/crew_follow_commands.py, reckon/crew/session_host.py and reckon/crew/follower_registration.py read them, and a worker launched from that session inherits them. No fixture in tests/conftest.py clears them. Measured 2026-10-21 by a population at merge (run r-20261021T104919664272-population-mon7, logs under /home/ITER/mcintos/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20261021T104919664272-population-mon7/env-inherited/): with them inherited, 86 tests failed at main 866fb9bf7, among them 13 in tests/test_crew_dispatch_guard.py, 10 in tests/test_crew_session_delivery.py and 7 in tests/test_a_reload_runs_the_committed_head.py. With them cleared (env-cleared/), 3 failed. So every worker gate over these files carries some 80 failures in both arms. They leave the delta intact but hide a real failure among them, and a worker must know to clear the variables for its gate to mean anything.

Deliverable. An autouse fixture in tests/conftest.py removes the four variables for every test, so a test that wants one sets it itself. A test that already sets one through monkeypatch.setenv keeps its value, because the fixture runs first.

Done when a new test, tests/test_the_suite_clears_the_follower_environment.py, runs a pytest subprocess over three files that fail at main with the variables inherited, tests/test_crew_dispatch_guard.py, tests/test_crew_session_delivery.py and tests/test_a_reload_runs_the_committed_head.py, with the four variables set to the values a crew host sets, and finds them passing, inside the repository's 300-second per-test bound. The whole inherited-environment population is the node's own two-arm gate rather than a test: an inherited run and a cleared run at head, from which reckon.crew.review.added_failures_from_gate_logs derives no added id, and any id failing in one environment only is run five times alone in each, because the population carries load-sensitive tests. The inherited-environment population is every test file with a FAILED or ERROR id in /home/ITER/mcintos/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20261021T104919664272-population-mon7/env-inherited/arm_base.log, the inherited arm at main, listed in the run's manifest. Every log is under the run directory, starts with its revision and the literal command, and ends in EXIT=. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings. §34 — Two stamp parsers go through the shared parser

Why. tests/test_structural_invariants.py::test_only_the_shared_parser_calls_fromisoformat fails at main. Two modules parse an ISO stamp with datetime.fromisoformat instead of the shared parser reckon/_timestamps.parse_utc: _created in reckon/crew/model_catalogue.py (line 64, from 7b4f6c828, 2026-10-20) and _review_dispatch_instant in reckon/crew/recovery_review_delivery.py (line 137, from b0050ee0f, 2026-10-19). A peer session found it and passed it to this plan, because both plans whose sections cite the test are closed.

Deliverable. Both functions parse their ISO strings through parse_utc, and no new exception is added to the test's list. _review_dispatch_instant already reads a zoneless stamp as UTC, so its results do not change. _created keeps its numeric branch as it is and sends only its string branch through parse_utc, so for strings it accepts exactly what parse_utc accepts. That is a deliberate change of contract, not a list of edge cases: every string parse_utc reads and datetime.fromisoformat did not, or reads differently, now follows parse_utc. The known ones are a zoneless string, now read as UTC rather than in the host's zone, which removes the result's dependence on TZ; a string padded with surrounding whitespace; and a lowercase z suffix. Both of the latter return None today and become readable. A string parse_utc cannot read still returns None.

Done when test_only_the_shared_parser_calls_fromisoformat passes at head, and a new test, tests/test_catalogue_stamps_read_through_the_shared_parser.py, holds a table of inputs that asserts _created(s) equals parse_utc(s).timestamp(), or None where parse_utc gives None, for every row. The rows cover a zoned stamp, a zoneless stamp under TZ=UTC and under TZ=Europe/Paris, a whitespace-padded stamp, a lowercase z suffix, an empty string and a malformed string. The new test fails at the base revision on the zoneless, padded and lowercase rows, run and logged there. The negative control restores one direct fromisoformat call, and the invariant then names that module. Every test file under tests/ importing either module adds no failure against a base arm, both arms from git checkouts under the run's own scratch directory with the four follower variables cleared, run in place on the fleet node with pytest -n 8. Every log is under the run directory, starts with its revision and the literal command, and ends in EXIT=. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings. Write paths: reckon/crew/model_catalogue.py, reckon/crew/recovery_review_delivery.py and the new test.

§37 — The follower delivery test waits for its reader

Why. tests/test_removed_flag_migration.py::test_the_delivery_path_itself_carries_no_state_filter fails intermittently, alone as well as under load. In five alone runs at base 6d186434 it failed twice, and in five at a head it failed once, each time with assert {'completed_unpromoted'} == {'completed_unpromoted', 'dispatched'}. That is, the reader received the run's terminal state and missed the earlier dispatched one (logs ~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20261021T204353308966-the-pane-repeats-no-line-that-carries-no-news/repeat-base2-removed_flag.log and repeat-head2-removed_flag.log). A failing run took 21 s against about 1 s for a passing one. The test starts a reader thread over cli._follow_watch_lines while the fixture moves two pointers from starting to delivered. Two windows could produce the miss, and the evidence so far does not tell them apart. The fixture may move the pointers before the reader has attached, so the reader's baseline already shows the later state; that is a defect of the test. Or the reader may miss a transition written between its baseline and its first read; that would be a defect of the follower, which would lose a real transition in production too. It fails in both arms of any population that includes it, so it reads as an added failure in about one arm in three.

Deliverable. First reproduce the failure in repeated alone runs and record the rate. Then find the window: whether the fixture moves the pointers before the reader has attached, or the reader can miss a transition written between its baseline and its first read. Fix the test so it waits for the reader to attach before the fixture moves anything, if that is the window, or fix the follower if a real transition can be lost, which would be a product defect and must be named as one.

Done when the test passes 20 of 20 alone runs and 10 of 10 under pytest -n 8 beside its file, in the foreground on the fleet node, and the other cases in tests/test_removed_flag_migration.py still pass. A repair made in the test alone must also show, by an instrumented run, that the reader attaches before the fixture moves anything and that no transition written after its baseline is missed. The manifest names the window with the responsible line read from the committed blob. The negative control reintroduces the window, by moving the pointers before the reader attaches, and the test then fails in at least one of ten runs. Every log is under the run directory, starts with its revision and the literal command, and ends in EXIT=. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings on the changed files. Write paths: tests/test_removed_flag_migration.py, and the follower module only if the manifest finds a lost transition.

§40 — Four tests red at main since 2026-10-22 pass again

Why. Four tests failed identically in every base and head arm the coordinator ran on 2026-10-23: at 9a9492e0a, at 77213b275 and at 579285757. They predate those runs, so each landing measured against them read zero added and left them red.

  • tests/test_mcp_views.py::test_live_runs_are_grouped_by_target_plan_and_project and tests/test_mcp_views.py::test_typed_plan_summary_reports_matching_live_run compare a live run row by equality. The rows now carry one more field, classification (for example 'running'). The shared run classification reached the rows on 2026-10-22, through f99ba9b33 and ba405e2c4.
  • tests/test_sprint_summary_lists_pending.py::test_summary_without_sprint_is_unchanged compares the summary view to a frozen JSON string, and the view has since gained fields.
  • tests/test_structural_invariants.py::test_only_the_shared_parser_calls_fromisoformat names project_brief.py:_parse_utc, a private ISO-stamp parser added in 4b499ff78, which composed the project brief.

Deliverable. For each of the first three, the commit that changed the behaviour is read to establish whether the change was intended. Where it was, the test asserts the new contract. The run-row tests expect the row's classification. The summary test exists to pin that the no-sprint summary gains nothing, so it stays a byte-exact comparison, re-frozen to the intended new bytes. Where it was not, the node changes neither the code nor that test. It reports the run blocked, naming the commit and the unintended change, and the coordinator writes the repair as its own section. reckon/project_brief.py's _parse_utc is replaced by reckon._timestamps.parse_utc, the one owner the invariant names. The two differ on a non-string stamp: _parse_utc returns None for 0 or 1700000000, and parse_utc reads them as epoch seconds. The brief's callers pass started_at, since and closed_at, which are ISO strings or absent. A non-string stamp is therefore treated as absent before parse_utc is called, so the brief keeps the accepted domain it has today, and a case asserts it.

Done when the four ids pass at head. Every test file that imports reckon.mcp_views or reckon.project_brief, with tests/test_sprint_summary_lists_pending.py and tests/test_structural_invariants.py, adds no failure against a base arm. The manifest names, for each of the first three, the commit read and whether its change was intended. Negative controls, one for each repair: restore project_brief.py's private parser, and the invariant test names it again; drop classification from the run row, and both run-row tests fail; add a field to the no-sprint summary, and the summary test fails; let a numeric stamp reach parse_utc, and the domain case fails. Each log names its revision and the literal command and ends in EXIT=, and baseline_suite and after_suite record failure_ids as pytest node ids. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings on the staged paths. The domain case goes in tests/test_one_brief_an_agent_can_act_on.py, the test file that imports reckon.project_brief. Write paths: tests/test_mcp_views.py, tests/test_sprint_summary_lists_pending.py, reckon/project_brief.py, tests/test_one_brief_an_agent_can_act_on.py, docs/evidence/fragments/the-suite-is-green-at-head/four-tests-red-at-main-pass-again.html.

test_tag_inventory reads a discovery memo another test leaves live

tests/test_tag_inventory.py::test_discovery_derives_tag_inventory_from_every_resource_type appeared as the only added id in two unrelated whole-suite head arms on 2026-09-30: a-review-manifest-passes-its-own-check (reports.py, r-20260930T140824474288) and a-review-never-runs-in-harness (recovery.py, r-20260930T143654216516). It passes in isolation, and with its suspected co-tenant. The first worker's account: the failure is a stale discovery inventory, which needs reckon/serve.py's _SIGNATURE_TTL_S memo (0.0 by default, :948) to be live. The only writer is serve.main(), which sets it to 60 s in a daemon thread inside tests/test_server_listens_before_it_watches.py. A leak past that test's monkeypatch teardown is a race whose visibility depends on which tests share an xdist worker, and adding any test file shifts that. Make the server test restore the memo TTL on teardown, or make discovery ignore a memo it did not arm, then prove it by running the pair in one worker in both orders. It may fold into load-sensitive-ids-hold-under-load.

/reckon-build the-suite-is-green-at-head §3

Landed 2026-10-01 at merges 5b197dc88 and 81b9f4bfe (run r-20260930T183005741612). The served_entry_point fixture joins the served thread before teardown returns and fails if it is still alive, then restores serve._SIGNATURE_TTL_S. A setup check refuses a case that starts with a live memo. With serve.main's write deferred until after shutdown, the base fixture left the tag-inventory test reading 60.0 and failing; with the join it reads 0.0 and passes. The control, the join removed, fails the thread-alive check, and promotion matched it. Reviewed at 82, 83 and 83 over three reworks. The done-when's teardown check cannot be written so that it can fail, because restore and check share one thread, so the setup check replaces it. That check catches a missing restore when served cases share a process, though not under -n with one served case per worker; this is stated in the code comments and accepted. The reworks looped because of the review-repair reflex, which s25 is capping at one automatic round per run. done — no followup.

Two pytest sessions given one --basetemp destroy each other

Reported by s22-coord on 2026-09-30, with the measurement at ~/.config/reckon/crew/reports/reckon/s22-coord/reflex-tests-environment/report.md and its arms beside it. At startup, pytest removes and recreates an explicit --basetemp. The session reaper in tests/conftest.py (reaped_watch_producers) then SIGTERMs every crew watch under that root. So a concurrent session sharing the directory loses its RECKON_HOME fixtures mid-run, and dispatch refuses with WatcherRequired. A repair gate using --basetemp=/tmp/repoa-head showed 17 failures across five reflex and lane modules. The same modules pass on a private basetemp: 49/49 in another worktree, and 50/50 at merged main. A controlled arm sharing the directory with a concurrent session reproduced one of the 17 ids through WatcherRequired, and the private control was green.

The fix refuses only the concurrent case, not sequential reuse, which workers rely on. A lock beside the basetemp is held for the session, and a second session starting under a basetemp whose lock a live pid holds is refused before anything is removed. The refusal names that pid. A stale lock whose pid is dead is taken over. Done when a case starts two sessions on one basetemp and the second is refused, naming the first's pid, with the first's files intact. A sequential second session on the same basetemp starts normally. The negative control (the lock removed) shows the first session's fixture directory deleted by the second. Write path: tests/conftest.py and a new test file. It is sequenced after the-suite-cannot-reach-a-real-scheduler, which holds tests/conftest.py.

/reckon-build the-suite-is-green-at-head §3

Landed at merge 32efb90e9 (run r-20261001T081400753558-a-shared-basetemp-is-refused, reviewed head 2225ab398, review total 90). tests/conftest.py takes a lock file beside an explicit --basetemp in pytest_configure and refuses a second session whose holder pid is live, before pytest removes the directory, naming the pid and the basetemp. A dead holder's lock is taken over, and a clean exit releases it. tests/test_a_shared_basetemp_is_refused.py drives three cases with real child sessions: refusal with the first session's fixtures intact, sequential reuse, and stale takeover. Head: 3 passed under -n 8. Base: 2 failed. The declared negative control is red. Coordinator population at the merge: the five files that pass --basetemp or spawn pytest (the new test, test_watch_arming_isolation, test_test_watchers_are_reaped, test_brief_prompt, test_standing_suite), run at main a67cf7d5f and at the merge, add no failing id. Both arms fail the same 2 pre-existing test_watch_arming_isolation ids. Logs are coord-population-{base,head}.log under the run's logs directory.

Make the basetemp lock exact: a reused pid, the create-to-write window, and the evidence fragment's head label

Three defects in the lock landed at 32efb90e9. (1) _process_is_alive treats any live pid as the holder, so a lock left by a killed session is never taken over once an unrelated process reuses that pid. The basetemp is then refused until someone removes the lock by hand. Record the holder's start time (field 22 of /proc/<pid>/stat) beside its pid, and treat a mismatch as stale. (2) acquire_basetemp_lock creates the file with O_EXCL and writes the pid afterwards. A second session that reads the still-empty file in that window gets None, unlinks the lock and takes it, so both sessions run. Write the content to a temporary sibling and os.link it into place, so the lock never exists without its holder. (3) docs/evidence/fragments/the-suite-is-green-at-head/a-shared-basetemp-is-refused.html line 44 names the base revision 531027092 for the head arm, whose revision is 2225ab398. Each of (1) and (2) needs a case in tests/test_a_shared_basetemp_is_refused.py, and a negative control under pytest must turn each case red.

/reckon-build the-suite-is-green-at-head §3

Landed at merge dfc087f41 (run r-20261001T093325066910-the-basetemp-lock-is-exact, reviewed head 455a72662, review total 91). The lock now records its holder's process start time beside the pid, and a live pid whose start time differs from the recorded one is treated as stale and taken over. acquire_basetemp_lock writes to a temporary sibling and links it into place, so the lock never exists without its holder. A lock read empty is refused while younger than five seconds. The evidence fragment names 2225ab398 for the head arm. tests/test_a_shared_basetemp_is_refused.py: 5 passed at head. Each declared mutation turns its own new case red. Coordinator population at the merge: the five files that pass --basetemp or spawn pytest show 2 failed at both main 26a2f77da and the merge, with identical pre-existing test_watch_arming_isolation ids. The review left two follow-ons, both bounded. First, the per-mutation control logs name the base revision for a head-derived scratch tree. Second, a lock written before this change carries no start time and is still refused if its pid is reused, which lasts until the next acquisition rewrites it. Neither was given a node. Stale takeover was also observed live: two whole-suite sessions stopped by SIGTERM left their locks, and the restarted sessions took both over.

A caveat on f-sgh-terminal-record-settle-reads-gpfs-mtime. The review of test-terminal-record-settle-at-the-merge (88) confirmed the attribution: the failure rate is the same at 87bcae7d and dc520696, and the case passes on xfs. It did not confirm the mechanism as a full account. The reviewer's replication on a GPFS basetemp read 14 of 20 utime-to-stat probes stale but failed only 2 of 13 runs. The node measured 42 of 60 stale probes and 11 of 20 failed runs. So the stale-mtime chain explains why a failing run's settle observes once, but it does not yet explain how often that happens. The repair should be judged by its pass counts on both filesystems, not by that mechanism alone.

review of r-20260929T183613683581-a-live-worker-still-owes-its-review at b63fe49d stored parsed 94/100 (goal_fidelity 19, evidence 18, scope_discipline 20, durability 18, fit 19) with added_failure_count 0 against the reviewed run's own base/head arms and four findings

ledger.write takes an exclusive fcntl.flock on a per-project lock file under crew_home()/locks and holds it across the entire read-modify-write -- the expected_version check, every per-run file write and the aggregate envelope write, plus the roster commit that can follow -- so two writers prepared from one revision are serialised rather than interleaved. A writer whose expected_version is stale is refused under the lock before it touches any per-run file, so the files a failing write puts back are only ever the ones it wrote itself; the interleaved schedule that previously put a stale snapshot over a committed row is no longer reachable, and the arriving writer is answered with the version conflict instead of a false conflict-of-history refusal. The lock path sits outside the tracked tree so it cannot dirty the repository it protects. The per-run files are still written before the envelope and still restored on failure, as landed in 1f87d481.

The independent review of r-20260929T092802902599-ledger-write-keeps-run-files (head ef5a9336b5e85c101dad35f98f8da46405a99bcb, base f1b9e06f33fb876704eec453a7264d02a362fccf) is stored at both granted paths, byte-identical (19246 bytes, sha256 92c4fc24…), reading status parsed with all five dimensions scored in 0..20 — goal_fidelity 19, evidence 19, scope_discipline 19, durability 19, fit 19, total 95 — carrying added_failure_count 0 with added_failure_ids [], four call sites, six item verdicts with none absent and no findings, and one prior-finding resolution: the earlier record's finding (reckon/ledger.py:993, a losing writer's restore putting the copy it captured over the winner's committed row) is resolved at head, the forced two-writer interleaving re-run red at the base and green at head, and the resolution shown to rest on the lock by a lock-removed head control. Decision — the shipped change (8259af866) is wrong and the fixtures are not stale: its host gate on the worker-record liveness read folded two distinct signals into one answer, and both test files model real shapes whose worker record must still be read. test_a_finished_run_never_reads_dispatched's fixtures carry the measured run's foreign launcher_host, and a record's presence proves its launch happened wherever it ran, so gating the phase advance on the reading host made a delivered foreign run fall back to dispatched. test_a_resumed_run_counts_as_working's fixtures carry no launcher_host — the shape a pointer written before that field existed has — and the resumed-attempt deferral reads the worker record precisely because the pointer's own pid cannot be resolved here, so refusing an unnamed host left a resumed run behind a live worker read as delivered and offered a promotion that would delete its live pointer. No fixture was edited (they model the measured inputs; moving them to name this host would erase the incident's regression test).
